<a id="the-theory-of-becoming"></a>
# The Theory of Becoming

Begun 9 October 2026 (Asia/Bangkok); restructured 20 October 2026 so that each thing is said once (the earlier edition is [kept in history](../history/THE-THEORY-OF-BECOMING-2026-10-20.md)). Direction: Yongxu Ren. The organization and wording are the AI's (Claude), from Ren's [priority proposal](../../../sources/15-codex-theory-conversations/2026-10-08-theory-of-becoming-priority.md), his [whole and manifold](../../../sources/15-codex-theory-conversations/2026-10-09-becoming-whole-generative-manifold.md), his [direction of 生生](../../../sources/15-codex-theory-conversations/2026-10-07-shengsheng-ethical-direction.md) (shēngshēng, “life giving rise to life”) and his instruction to [unify everything as one theory](../../../sources/15-codex-theory-conversations/2026-10-09-one-theory-unification.md). These are provenance; the argument is given here. Ren's own words are marked as quotations; everything else is the AI's.

This chapter is the canonical entry to the theory. It states every premise once (§0), the argument once (§1–§7), each result once in [the theorem map](#the-theorem-map), what is open once (§8) and the Lean artefact once (§9). Earlier chapters keep their own arguments at their original scope (§11).

<a id="一句话--in-one-sentence"></a>
<a id="in-one-sentence"></a>
<a id="preface"></a>
## Preface

**In one sentence.** The Theory of Becoming explains how relations form, persist, transform, degenerate and dissolve within one relational unfolding, and how life, evolution, knowing and evaluation differentiate within it, without first assuming that becoming is good.

**Four kinds of standing.** Every statement below carries one of these:

- **theorem without premise**: holds of every world so represented, with no premise about the unfolding; any local hypothesis it needs (finiteness, enclosure, listing, decidedness) is written into it, and the representation's commitments are those of §0, block A;
- **theorem under stated premises**: holds where the premises it names hold (a World premise of §0, block C, a correspondence of block D, or a practical premise of block F);
- **the condition**: the continuation condition (`Unrestricted`, §6.4), which the theory states and does not assert;
- **reading**: an interpretation of checked content, adopted with its argument and marked **[AI reading, Dn]**, D*n* being its entry in the [decision register](../../../_process/research/2026-10-05-generative-foundation-review/root-foundation/DELEGATED-CHOICES.md). A reading changes no theorem, and a better argument revises it.

What is neither is **open** (§8). Every theorem is checked in Lean with an empty axiom report (§0, block A; §9).

**Where the ethics lies.** The ethics rests on two premises with two roles, and neither is asserted.
- *Scope: the continuation condition.* For anything, its continuation has significance. It fixes whose losses count, and by itself it orders nothing: mixed parts stay unordered, and every narrowing loses something (§6.4).
- *Order: C, the widening comparison.* Widening what can form is better. C is the only premise that ranks courses (§6.5). That widening is an order needs no premise (§6.1); that it is better is C.
- *The step of invariance.* The condition is exactly the self's presupposition joined to the step that significance stops at no boundary. That step is the condition's content beyond the self, and it extends the scope to losses that return to no one; it ranks nothing (§6.5).
So no ethical result follows from the condition alone: what counts comes from the condition, what ranks from C, and their warrant is open (§8.5).

<a id="central-results"></a>
**Central results.** Ten results carry the argument; each is stated in the section given and listed in the theorem map.

1. *One change, three readings, and keeping pace* (§1.2). Arising and ceasing, a dependence forming and dissolving, and a condition coming to stand and ceasing to stand are one change; in a finite family coming to be and ceasing keep pace, with no premise.
2. *Recurrence and attraction* (§1.2). Within finitely many configurations a law merges nothing exactly where every configuration returns; a configuration is drawn into an absorbing region exactly where it does not return; keeping one's history needs an open world.
3. *Premises carve parts* (§1.3). The eight World premises are each met by some worlds and failed by others; read off one exchange, five hold by construction and three are states of the law of exchange.
4. *Rest needs a least loss or a finite reading* (§1.3). Rest follows from a least loss per change and a stock within finitely many of them, or, with no least loss, from an amount read off finitely many configurations; approach to rest needs only Archimedean amounts; a law that merges nothing returns rather than rests, and a part's merge inside it is kept by the rest.
5. *The order of what can form* (§6.1). With no premise, a part widens, narrows, keeps or is mixed; it closes something only by a net draw beyond its margin and opens something only by a net give.
6. *Closures and selection* (§2.6, §3.4). Feeding, influence, reach and system are nested closures; a selection through couplings reaches exactly what couples in, and a pool selects through the union of its members' couplings.
7. *Joint excess and relative position* (§2.3, §4.3). Takers each within what is given can together exceed it and end the renewers they depend on; relative position sums to zero by counting.
8. *Learning and identification* (§3.3). No finite prefix fixes the next value; within a bound of N states a prefix of 2N − 1 steps fixes the course; learning through a map is tracking in the limit.
9. *Significance is not in the dynamics* (§5, §6.4–§6.5). No law of the dynamics fixes significance; the condition is the self's presupposition with the step of invariance, and it orders nothing.
10. *Loops* (§2.6). The most difference that can arrive grows faster than every polynomial only through a loop of couplings that go on, and such a loop need not.

None of the ten is new mathematics: each is near-definitional or a known result restated in the theory's vocabulary, and [the relation to known results](#relation-to-known-results) names the closest prior result of each.

**Beyond the theorems.** The theory's strongest claims about actual systems, AI and ethics, each at full strength with its standing, what it forbids and what would refute it, are in [theses and conjectures](#theses-and-conjectures): persistence certifies the joint draw; the state of a renewable condition under a draw; optimization drains what lies beyond its couplings where that gives room; the scope can be installed; the order is shared by every valuer; a break is seen coming only through what couples into what breaks; a measure read from what it selects outlives its refutation; and the philosophical theses. A thesis is not a theorem, and its standing is stated with it.

**Two targets, kept apart.** *The deductive necessity of the whole*: that the whole's determinations are internally related moments of one unfolding, with no added substantive premise. A theorem of every world so represented is not thereby necessary; this target is open (§8.1, items 1 and 12). *The ethical orientation within the whole*: how 生生 (life in the whole and its continuing generation) acquires normative significance, through C under the continuation condition, with the practical premises disclosed. Under its premises this target is complete in a thin sense: they are stated and what follows is checked, and much of it unfolds a definition (`Web.paths_comparison_widens`, `livingSignificance_of_unrestricted`); the warrant of the two premises is open. Neither target substitutes for the other: an absence of ethical axioms is not an absence of substantive premises, and an empty axiom report is not an absence of premises. Objections not yet answered are in §8.9.

**Scope.** The theory counts the whole space of becoming, generative and degenerative alike. Generation is one part of it, not a condition every change must meet; degeneration, ending and mixed change are in the same unfolding and are not renamed generation ([the foundation's scope](BECOMING-FOUNDATION.md#whole-and-manifold)).

<a id="0-前提卡--premise-card"></a>
## 0. Premise card

Every premise is named here and nowhere else explained. In Lean each is a definition or a hypothesis, never an axiom. The first two blocks are not premises about the unfolding: they are how it is represented, and the local hypotheses individual theorems carry. Block C carves parts of the space; blocks D–F bear on application, ethics and practice.

**A. Commitments of the representation**

| Commitment | Content | Lean | Standing |
|---|---|---|---|
| Proof language | Lean 4.29.1 kernel. The core imports only Lean's `Init` (the prelude); the checker strips the foundation's `import Std` and recompiles the core closure without it. Every source declaration has an empty axiom report: no `propext`, `Quot.sound` or `Classical.choice`. Constructivity needs only the absence of `Classical.choice` (propositional extensionality and quotient soundness are constructively acceptable), so the core is **stricter than constructive**: no excluded middle, choice, propositional or function extensionality is assumed. What identifying interderivable propositions (`propext`) keeps and loses, and why the core states `↔` where a result would need `=`, is stated in §9 (`Becoming.PropositionIdentity`). A theorem that needs a classical principle names it as a hypothesis (excluded middle, LPO, WLPO, LLPO, Markov's principle, double-negation elimination, dependent choice), and §8.6 says what each need means for the unfolding. **The axiom report concerns logic only.** It shows that no logical axiom is used; it does not show that the rows below hold of any actual unfolding, and the substantive assumptions lie there. The periphery may use Lean's standard axioms, each result with its own receipt (§9). The provenance of the requirement is [Ren's pure-core request](../../../sources/15-codex-theory-conversations/2026-10-09-pure-core.md). | `check_becoming.py` (prelude-only step) | method commitment |
| Discrete steps, one shared index | Every relatum's activity, level and exchange is indexed by one sequence of natural-number steps. No definite causal order beyond it is assumed (§1.4). For exchanges acting locally the index is read off the relata's own courses; beyond them it orders only exchanges sharing no relatum, which changes no configuration (§1.4). What is read through a relatum's own steps is the same for every interleaving (`Interleavings.LocalLaw.world_index`, `world_reads_alike`). Where every exchange touches a relatum, one renumbering of all the steps carries one interleaving's world to the other's (`renumber_reindexes`), and a relation read at one step on the relata that step's exchange touches, however many, is the same for every interleaving (`world_renumber_reads`); a relation read at one step on relata no exchange there connects is read on the interleaving chosen (`Pair.apart_differs`, §1.4). | `World`, `Web`, `Flow` | representation |
| Natural-number amounts | Levels, needs, takes and gives are natural numbers; a positive amount is at least one unit. Dissipation's least loss, the doubling of loops and novelty needing gain depend on it. The rest theorems (`World.comes_to_rest_unrefuted`, `World.rests_unrefuted_enclosed`, `World.rests_unrefuted`), feeding from beyond (`Flow.fed_from_beyond`), and three theorems with no premise about the unfolding (`rising_recurs`, `falls_not_recurring`, `Flow.excess_bounded`) sum falls of at least one; over dense amounts a stock halving at every step falls for ever and never rises (`Halving.approaches_without_reaching`). Their general form is the least-loss law (`Amounts.rests`), of which `falls_not_recurring` is the whole-number instance (§1.3); read in any amounts, the rest theorem needs exactly that the census's total stop falling (`World.InAmounts.change_stops_iff`, `World.InAmounts.rests_unrefuted_enclosed_of_stops`), which a finite reading or a least fall in Archimedean amounts gives, and along an amount falling at every step a world need not rest (`Pass.endless_keeps_from_rest`). The second law supplies no least loss per step. | `Web.level`, `Flow.take`, `Flow.give` | representation; finer units in `Becoming.Units`, `Becoming.Resolution`; dense amounts in `Becoming.Amounts`, each premise needed (`Becoming.LeastLoss`), worlds in any amounts in `Becoming.InAmounts` |
| Prop-valued activity | Activity is a proposition, `active : Nat → F → Prop`. Laws that count or name a step need it decided; asked of every world without decidedness some laws are exactly excluded middle, a calibration of this commitment (§8.6). | `Web.active` | representation |
| One kind of relatum | A world is relata of one kind, with a flow (`present`, `take`, `give`) and a web (`level`, `active`, `requires`, `need`, `draws`). Formations and conditions are two roles of one relatum. | `World`, `World.ofRoles`, `Becoming.Roles` | representation |
| The shared index | Which condition is which relatum is given by the representation, not derived from the dynamics. | `relabel` | representation premise |
| Requirements as thresholds | What a formation requires at a step, and how much, is given in advance for every relatum of the world's type. Support is meeting every requirement: `need ≤ level`. | `Web.requires`, `Web.need`, `Web.Supported` | representation |
| Context-free claims (frames only) | A frame reads what is shown of a claim as independent of what is shown with it. What quantum states show does not meet this. | `Becoming.Context` | commitment of the frame view (§3.3) |
| Identity across steps | Which formation continues which is fixed by the naming. | `Becoming.Identity` | representation (§2.2) |
| Pointwise properties | The decided counting laws are stated for properties of counts that respect their values at each step, since the core does not assume function extensionality. Over counted censuses no such condition is needed: counts taken as relations are unique where they exist (`Counts.unique`, `Becoming.CensusCounts`). | `Pointwise` | hypothesis of the decided counting laws |

**B. Local hypotheses carried by individual theorems**

| Hypothesis | Content | Lean | Where it is used |
|---|---|---|---|
| Finite | Finitely many relata, each listed once | `World.Finite` | finite-world theorems; a finite world has a census where its activity is decided, and every step of it is enclosed |
| Enclosed | Across a step, the exchange of the formations taking part lies within some finite family without repetition; around a family or around what stands, the same with that family included | `Flow.Enclosed`, `Flow.Around`, `AroundStanding` | rest, change-bound and drawing-in theorems |
| Listed | What holds at each step is listed once; a family is without repetition | `Listed`, `Web.Census`, `Distinct` | census, counting and loop theorems |
| Decided | What a theorem counts or names is decided where it needs it. A count exists exactly where what it counts is decided (`counts_iff_decided`); listing once is decided identity (`listsOnce_iff_decided`). The counting laws of `Becoming.Counting` take `[∀ t x, Decidable (w.web.active t x)]`, global decidability of activity; over counted censuses (`Becoming.CensusCounts`, `Becoming.CensusBinding`) the laws under the premises, each premise subset, enclosure, finite worlds and where the premises bind take none, a ceasing or coming count across a step existing exactly where being listed at the other end is weakly decided (`World.ceasings_exist_iff_listed`). | `Becoming.Decidedness`, `Becoming.Listing` | counting, recurrence and naming-a-step theorems |

**C. The World premises**

| Premise | Content | Lean |
|---|---|---|
| Activity needing support | A relatum is active only where the levels meet everything it then requires | `World.Dependent` (`Web.Dependent`) |
| Balance | Every change of a level is exactly what is taken from it and given to it | `World.Balanced` (`Flow.Balances`) |
| Only active relata give | Whatever gives across a step is active there | `World.Acting` (`Flow.Acting`) |
| Only active relata take, and only from what they draw on | Whatever takes across a step is active there and draws on what it takes from | `World.Taking` (`Flow.Taking`) |
| Dissipation | On every family without repetition closed across a step, each formation taking part gives it no more than it takes from it, and at least one unit less when it takes: a least loss of one whole unit per taking step (block A); over dense amounts the least loss is a premise of the rest law | `World.Dissipative` (`Flow.Dissipative`, `Flow.DissipatesOn`) |
| Embodiment | Every relatum stands as a condition exactly while it is active | `World.Embodied` |
| A census | The relata active at each step are listed, each once | `World.Counted` |
| Bounded transmission | Descent transmits every dependence within one bound | `World.Transmitting` |

Two weaker forms are used: a reserve carrying activity (`World.Carried`) and embodied arising (`World.ArisingEmbodied`). Activity needing support gives the zero reserve; embodiment gives embodied arising; a finite world with decided activity has a census; with only active taking and dissipation, only active relata give across an enclosed step (`World.acting_across`).

- *Contingent and independent.* Seven premises (the first six and the reserve) are each met by some worlds and failed by others (`World.contingent`); the eight of the table are independent, each failed by a world meeting the other seven (`World.independent`, `World.none_follows`). Finiteness, enclosure and decidedness are not among them. In `hatch` all eight hold and each binds, binding meaning only that the premise's condition has an instance (`World.hatch_binds`); no finite world binds the census, by definition (`World.Finite.not_binds`).
- *What the independence measures.* The freedom of `World`'s separately chosen fields, not contingency in becoming. Read off one exchange, with the rest of the whole counted as one relatum, the eight divide in two (`Becoming.Reading`, `Becoming.ExchangePremises`).
  - *Five hold by construction.* Balance, only active giving and only active taking hold of a world read off an exchange; for a law of exchange, so does activity needing support (`World.withRest`, `ExchangeLaw.readWorld_glue`). This does not remove the premise: with the rest counted, balance holds exactly where the rest is never active (`World.withRest_closed_iff`), and under a law of what has stood the rest is idle exactly where the course is feasible, no exchange taking from a condition more than stands on it and is given to it (`ExchangeLaw.readWorld_closed_iff`, `ExchangeLaw.course_balances_iff`). Nothing comes from nothing is then a premise located in the law, feasibility. A census holds wherever a named family closes each step's exchange, with no decision procedure (`World.withRest_counted`, `World.Finite.withRest_counted`); without enclosure no world read off an exchange fails a census at any step, but asking one at every step of every flow over `Nat` is exactly LPO (`Flow.not_not_listed_at`, `Flow.listed_iff_lpo`).
  - *Three are states of the law of exchange.* Worlds read off laws, with the rest counted and idle and the four tying premises holding, meet and fail each (`ExchangeLaw.contingent`). Dissipation is loss (`responsive`); embodiment is no dormancy (`still`; with the rest counted, embodiment is exactly balance and each relatum exchanging exactly while it stands, `World.withRest_embodied_iff`); bounded transmission is heredity of response (`sprouting`). As closure is feasibility, enclosure is the law's reach (`ExchangeLaw.spreading_not_enclosed`, `ExchangeLaw.spreading_counted`).
  - *Requirement over open exchanges.* Where a relation leaves several exchanges open, requirement read as necessary response gives activity needing support on every run (`ExchangeRelation.reading_glue`); read as possible response it does not (`ExchangeRelation.free_unsupported`). For a law of exchange the two readings are the law's (`ExchangeLaw.relation_readWeb_requires`, `ExchangeLaw.relation_readWeb_may`).

Records: [map-of-premises](../results/records/map-of-premises.md), [premises-read-off-the-exchange](../results/records/premises-read-off-the-exchange.md), [census-read-off-the-exchange](../results/records/census-read-off-the-exchange.md), [premises-that-are-states-of-the-law](../results/records/premises-that-are-states-of-the-law.md), [requirement-over-open-exchanges](../results/records/requirement-over-open-exchanges.md).

| Layer | Premise | Content | Lean | Standing |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Dynamics | Process | Admissible changes `step s a t` | `Generative.Process` | a view of the one unfolding, the move read within the configuration (§1.1); in an application, which process is read is part of the correspondence |
| Correspondence | Observables | Claims realized, condition levels, dependence and drawing correspond to actual histories | `Frame.realized`, `Conditions` | application obligation |
| Correspondence | Formation E | The lives and relations evaluated actually form within the unfolding | — | application obligation |
| Correspondence | Connection | Living claims are joined through formed relations | `Valuation.Connected` | given by common conditions under the discipline of correspondence between conditions and participation; interaction (one draws on a condition and the other depends on it at a positive level) gives connection in the declared class (§6.2) |
| Correspondence | Readings of `living` | Whole-run (active and not consuming), stepwise (active and not undermining) and relation level, each relative to a run. They read activity compatible with others' dependence, not further generation. | `Conditions.valuation`, `Web.valuation`, `Web.relations` | a disclosed choice; no comparison uses it. 生生 is read as generation generating generation, compared by widening [AI reading, D1]; the `living` readings do not read 生生 |
| Correspondence | Held | Every living claim lies in some standpoint's own region (always, on the stepwise reading where each formation's region is its own events); that holding is decided is `Valuation.significant_iff_reached`'s own hypothesis | `Valuation.Held` | the formal reading of “what wants its continuation”; self-significance reads “wanting” [AI reading, D11] (§6.2) |

**E. Ethics** (none asserted)

| Premise | Content | Lean | Standing |
|---|---|---|---|
| **The continuation condition** | For anything, its continuation at every step has significance from every standpoint. The wording is Ren's proposal, kept as provenance: “for anything, its continuation into the future has significance” ([source](../../../sources/15-codex-theory-conversations/2026-10-10-wanting-continuation-one-axiom.md)). | `Unrestricted` (`∀ p t f, sig p t f`) | **Stated, not asserted** (§6.4). It fixes scope: whose losses count. It gives significance, not a positive valence, and orders nothing. Living significance, the seed, self-significance and the two neutralities follow from it by restriction; each such theorem is an instantiation of a universal statement (§6.4). C does not follow from it [AI reading, D16]. |
| **Living significance** | Every living claim has significance from every standpoint; on a frame, realized living content falls under the relevance read from significance | `LivingSignificance`, `Valuation.Significant` | A condition, not an axiom. Characterized exactly with no premise (`Valuation.significant_iff`, `Valuation.significant_iff_held`; §6.2); not entailed by the dynamics (`Web.significance_undetermined`; §5); the condition restricted to living claims (§6.4). Its binder `relevance` is a parameter, not a valence (`relevance_least`). |
| Self-significance | A standpoint's own living claims have significance from it | `SelfSignificance` → `Seed` | A normative premise reading “wanting one's continuation” [AI reading, D11]; living claims only; everywhere under the condition (§6.4). It is not the seed, which any standpoint holding self-significance with its own living content supplies. |
| Relational neutrality | Significance passes along formed relations to living content (the side of the claim reached) | `transmits` | a boundary-neutrality premise; follows from the condition |
| Standpoint neutrality | What has significance from any standpoint has it from every standpoint (the side of the standpoint) | `shares` | a boundary-neutrality premise; follows from the condition. Where every living claim is held, living significance is exactly self-significance with standpoint neutrality; on a connected whole where every standpoint holds a living claim, self-significance with relational neutrality also gives it (§6.2) [AI reading, D11] |
| A (articulation) | Access, together with securing positive content from one's own standpoint over one's scope, gives a supporting reason (the side of the position addressed) | `articulates` | an externalist articulation premise; under the condition it must be read on living content, since on bare significance it would give a reason for any new content [AI reading, D16] |

**F. Practice**

| Premise | Content | Lean | Standing |
|---|---|---|---|
| C (the widening comparison) | An extension with no loss that realizes more is better; with “better” read as widening what can form, C's no-loss extension is exactly widening (`Web.paths_comparison_widens`, definitional) | `Comparison` | **The order premise, the only one that ranks courses** (§6.5). Courses are compared on possibilities: extension on possibilities ranks the absence of an overdrawing formation above its presence and withholds no event (`Ranking.possible_extends_iff`). Under the condition widening is retaining and adding what has significance (`Web.widens_iff_retains_adds`); reading “better” that way is a reading, not a consequence of the condition. That widening is better is the open warrant. |
| L (lifting) | Lifting the comparison of outcomes to responses with many outcomes; the conservative default is “every outcome is better than every outcome” | `LiftBetter` | a substantive commitment (§6.5) |
| J1–J4, the practical commitments | Complete comparison and retained losses; priority of basic conditions; bounded responsibility; legitimate feasible choice | `Lexical.BasicPriority`, `Lexical.Frame.Above` (J1/J2), `Requirement.Frontier`, `Required` (J3/J4); on a frame `FrontierFrames.Priority`, `FrontierFrames.Options`; J3 on a world's moves by `FrontierWorlds` | commitments proposed by the AI, conditions of the practical theorems; a choice at every step needs weak excluded middle (`FrontierFrames.choice_exists_iff_weakly`, §8.6) |
| FR | Finite responsiveness: within an independently specified family of recoverable basic losses of one kind, a fixed graver loss does not keep sufficient priority over arbitrarily many fixed lesser basic protections by its gravity alone | `Becoming.LossMatching` (the formal content of strict loss matching, not the warrant) | an additional, revisable comparison commitment with affirmative defence and counter-review; neither theorem nor axiom ([finite-aggregation-within-restorable-basic-losses](../results/records/finite-aggregation-within-restorable-basic-losses.md)) |

Other named commitments of the practical line (BC1, BC2, LC, LR, CP, NP) are stated with their records in §6.5.

**Not assumed.** That becoming is good; inevitable improvement or universal growth; a fixed common utility; independent owners of value; that every change is generative; a definite global causal order at every interface (the representation has one shared index of steps, and what the theorems use of it is the order of change, §1.4).

<a id="第一部分不用公理--part-i-without-axiom"></a>
<a id="the-theory-without-axiom-sections-16"></a>
<a id="1-生成同一展开--becoming-one-unfolding"></a>
## 1. Becoming: one unfolding

Sections 1–6 hold everything that needs no ethical premise: theorems of every world so represented, and, for evaluation, characterizations that hold for any significance. They stand whatever is decided about the warrant of the continuation condition, which enters only in §6.4. Premises appear only as hypotheses of the theorems that name them.

### 1.1 One unfolding and its views

**In words.** The start is one unfolding of relations: relata of one kind, with what each takes, gives, stands at, needs and draws on at each step. Process, frame and world are views of it, and each returns to it: a move is part of the configuration, a law is read off the history it governs, a claim is a relatum, and a configuration is how it relates. The base is one, the run; a world is a run by its type.

- *A history fixes the law it shows.* Every history is a run of the least law it is a run of, and what a law given before the history adds are only the moves not taken, which no record fixes (`shown_le_iff`, `shown_determined_iff`, `exists_law_iff`). Laws up to their dead ends correspond one to one with families of histories holding their splices (`family_iff`, `same_histories_iff`). Used in §2.6 (the counterfactual is the law's step beyond its history) and §3.1.
- *The move is part of the unfolding.* Read within the configuration the move changes no run, and the law leaves the next move open exactly where it was determined before with one move at each step (`withMove_determined_iff`). A world's move is its relata's exchange: a world balances exactly where its levels run by the law of exchange with the exchange as the move (`World.balances_iff_exchange`, by definition).
- *A world is a run, by its type.* A `World` is a run of the process admitting every step, whose configuration at each step is what the world is then and whose move is its exchange; `Web F C` is `Dependence Nat F C`, and the two views are one by `rfl` (`World.run_world`, `Run.world_webAt`). A determined law's notions (merging, returning, reachability, keeping every possibility) are its process's by definition (`Law.lossless_iff`, `Law.returns_iff`). Used throughout: one theorem about runs serves worlds.
- *A configuration is how it relates.* Configurations related by a bisimulation are alike, and a description makes sense exactly where it identifies the alike configurations; it is then unique and, where the frame is determined up to likeness, carries a law (`Alike.tests`, `identifiesAlike_iff`, `IdentifiesAlike.carries`). Used in §3.3 (理 as a lumping the world goes alike across).
- *Separation loses the relation.* Two parts are a pair by relation: describing them separately gives the whole back exactly where the whole is rectangular, and otherwise loses exactly the relation, which reappears as extra combinations; three parts can be related while every two are free (`separation_faithful_iff`, `separation_admits_extra`, `whole_not_in_pairs`).

The views in context always form one world, and a context-free description exists exactly where they glue (`Contextual.world`, `Contextual.fitsFree_iff`): `World` is the view where the views glue, not the start. Where each context requires a parity, a context-free reading exists exactly where no selection of contexts contradicts; then a mixture of context-free readings meets at most m − 1 of the m contexts on average, and readings in context meet all m (`ParitySystem.glues_iff`, `ParitySystem.mixture_le`; Specker's three boxes, the CHSH game and the Mermin–Peres square are instances). A condition's change is read by its standing and by the exchange alike exactly where the flow balances, and with the rest counted the two readings glue at every step (`Flow.changeReadings_glue_iff`, `World.unity`). A run of a process whose configurations are not webs is a world only through its claims, with loss (`Frame.world`, `Frame.world_fixes_run`). Not derived: the shared index of steps; whether a move is formed within the unfolding, and by what (§2.6, `Becoming.MoveFormation`); which law a formation takes its history to show; that configurations passing the same tests are alike (shown where frames are determined up to likeness, `alike_iff_sameTests`, and constructively for finite frames with decided steps, `alike_iff_sameBranchingTests`).

*Readings* [AI reading, D23, D32, D33, D43]. Law and history are one unfolding read two ways, the law read off the unfolding rather than set over it; the gap between law and history is memory, and the splice is memory's absence. Action is not a second kind of cause beside the unfolding; knowing lies within it; the names of configurations carry nothing. The root is the run: every view of the unfolding is a run. Two are a pair by relation, in what they can be together or in how they go on together (under a law a pair is a split that makes sense, `Motion.neither_alone`); the members are read off the pair, and a pair is the least split, not the least relation.

### 1.2 Theorems with no premise: change, return and attraction

**In words.** A determination comes to hold or ceases to hold across a step, and that one change, read of activity, dependence and standing, is arising and ceasing, a dependence forming and dissolving, and a condition coming to stand and ceasing to stand. Over any stretch the end with its falls equals the start with its rises, so in a finite family coming to be and ceasing keep pace. A law that merges nothing returns every configuration of a finite world; what does not return is drawn into a region it never leaves; and what a course loses it loses at a juncture.

- `change_readings` (by `Iff.rfl`) and `over_stretch`: the one change and the tally, with no premise. With amounts natural numbers, finitely many conditions that keep falling keep rising (`rising_recurs`); with finitely many formations and activity decided, arising recurs exactly when ceasing does (`World.arising_iff_ceasing`); endless novelty can arise without ceasing (`Accretion`) and endless members cease without arising (`Attrition`), so finiteness does the work. Used in §1.3 and §2.1.
- `Law.lossless_iff_returns`: within finitely many configurations a determined law merges nothing exactly where every configuration returns; keeping the history admits only that history, at the price that it never returns (`Run.keepHistory_shown`, `Becoming.Recurrence`). Used in §1.3 (rest belongs to laws that merge).
- `Law.drawn_iff_not_returns`: a configuration is drawn into some absorbing region exactly where it does not return; what is drawn in never comes back (`Law.DrawnInto.never_back`), and what can be had n steps on is an absorbing region into which every possibility lost by step n is drawn (`Law.reachable_absorbing`, `Law.lost_drawn`). Within finitely many configurations attraction is merging (`Law.lossless_iff_none_drawn`); an open world separates them (`Law.succ_drawn_lossless`, `Law.pred_drawn_keeps`). Used in §3.4 (capture is an absorbing region in the rule) and §4.4.
- `necessarilyDrawn_iff_not_returns`: under branching every process necessarily draws in exactly what does not return; keeping every possibility no longer gives returning (`Transit.keeps_not_returns`), and a merge need not lose a possibility (`OneWay.merge_keeps`).
- `loss_passes_juncture`: where `s` returns, every course from `s` to where `s` can no longer be had passes a juncture, up to double negation; finding it is deciding whether `s` can still be had, found where a list holds the configurations (`loss_passes_juncture_listed`) and, for every process, exactly LPO (`lpo_of_junctures_found`). Keeping only the steps after which `s` can still be had is the greatest sub-process keeping `s` (`keeping_greatest`).

Three further theorems complete the line. *Every origin passes the gate* (`Becoming.Gated`): where the steps not marked by a decided gate reflect a property, a course ending where it holds started where it held or passes a gated step after which it holds, nothing about the property decided (`Gated.origin`); with the gate a proposition, asked of every process this is double-negation elimination (§8.6). *Where a run goes on* (`Becoming.Onward`): within finitely many configurations a run starts exactly where a course as long as the list starts, exactly where a course leads to a return, with no decision and no choice (`run_iff_continues`, `run_iff_returning`); where configurations are unbounded a run is built by search where a test recognizes the moves (`searchedRun`); asked of every process it is exactly dependent choice (`runs_iff_dependentChoice`). *What a commuting law keeps apart* (`Becoming.Commuting`): a law commuting with a map that moves every configuration never brings a configuration and its image together (`Law.Commutes.apart`), so merging a difference a symmetry carries needs breaking the symmetry (`Law.Commutes.breaks`).

*Readings* [AI reading, D25, D32, D43, D52]. Lock-in is not returning: an attractor is the unfolding narrowing its own future. Within finitely many configurations whatever goes on without end does so by coming round, read off the unfolding with nothing chosen. 机 is a juncture; 护闭 is keeping and 成闭 losing at a juncture, one operation with no valuation, and many options do not make a juncture. A law that keeps a symmetry never erases the difference the symmetry carries. Which regions actual unfoldings enter, and a measure on branching courses, are not derived.

### 1.3 Premises carve parts: counting, rest and openness

**In words.** The theorems with no premise hold of every world; each premise of §0, block C, carves a proper part of the space, and what holds of a part follows from the premises that carve it. Counted, coming and ceasing balance on one list. Rest belongs to the part that dissipates with a least loss per change and a stock within finitely many such losses, or whose amounts are read off finitely many configurations; continued change needs openness; and what goes on without end is fed from beyond every bounded part.

- *Counting where the counts exist* (`World.counting_under_counted`): a count is a relation existing exactly where what it counts is decided (`counts_iff_decided`, `World.ceasings_exist_iff_listed`); wherever the counts of a census exist they are a tally, and under each premise subset, under enclosure and in finite worlds the laws of counting hold of them for every property of the counts. With decided activity, under each of the 256 premise subsets the counting laws are the tally alone or the tally with nothing arising at a step where nothing is active (`World.counting_under`). Under a bounded census arising and ceasing recur together even among infinitely many relata; asked of every finite world without decidedness the law is exactly excluded middle (`World.law_iff_em`), a calibration of Prop-valued activity and a lemma of the decided law.
- *The rest theorem* (`World.comes_to_rest_unrefuted`): in a balanced, dissipative world with censuses of what is active and of what stands, whose arising is embodied and which is enclosed around what stands from some step on, that it comes to rest cannot be refuted; each premise is needed (`leaking`, `attrition`, `throughput`), and naming the step after which nothing arises is LPO (`World.lpo_of_finite_ends`). What brings rest is finitely many standing and active at each step with enclosure, not finiteness of the world (`rest_few`). Used in §2.3.
- *Rest needs a least loss or a finite reading* (`Amounts.rests`): over amounts that may be dense, falls of at least a least loss from a stock within finitely many of them do not recur; whole numbers are the instance, and the rest theorem is proved from it (`Amounts.rests_unrefuted`, through `Flow.change_stops_unrefuted`, `World.rests_unrefuted_enclosed`). Each premise is needed (`Halving.never_rests`, `Layered.never_rests`). Approach to rest needs only Archimedean amounts (`Amounts.few_large_losses_of_archimedean`, `Amounts.approaches_rest`), as exponential relaxation shows (`Halving.approaches_without_reaching`). Over finitely many configurations a loss above nothing needs a law that merges (`Law.loss_needs_merge`): rest belongs to descriptions that lump configurations and dissipate. Read off configurations a list holds, rest needs no least loss: an amount that does not rise falls strictly at most as often as the list is long, over any amounts (`Amounts.rests_of_finite_reading`; along a run of every process `Branching.rests_along_run`), so falls that recur need a reading no list holds, as the halving stock's (`Halving.stock_unlisted`). Inside a law that merges nothing, a part's merge is kept by the rest: possibilities kept apart number at most the part's count times the rest's (`Law.Lossless.narrowing_spreads`), so a part reset at every step needs a whole no list holds (`Law.Lossless.reset_unlisted`; `Reset.kept_by_rest`). This is Landauer's count, with no energy ([rest-from-a-finite-reading](../results/records/rest-from-a-finite-reading.md), [merge-of-a-part-kept-by-the-rest](../results/records/merge-of-a-part-kept-by-the-rest.md)). The rest theorem itself holds over any amounts read finitely. Read a world's standings, takes and gives in any amounts (`World.InAmounts`): balanced (no amount made) and dissipated on (whatever gives back all it takes takes nothing), a family's total does not rise and falls strictly at every change of a level, with no least loss (`World.InAmounts.change_lowers`); so where the census's total is read off configurations a list holds, a world with a census, every relatum embodied, balanced, dissipative and enclosed from some step on rests, unrefuted (`World.InAmounts.rests_unrefuted_enclosed`; covered by one family `World.InAmounts.rests_unrefuted_covering`, finite `World.InAmounts.rests_unrefuted`). Its one premise is that the census's total stops falling: a family balanced and dissipated on stops changing exactly where its total stops falling (`World.InAmounts.change_stops_iff`), and a world whose census's total stops falling rests (`World.InAmounts.rests_unrefuted_enclosed_of_stops`). A finite reading gives it, and so do amounts in which no amount falls without end, which a least fall in Archimedean amounts gives (`Amounts.noEndlessFall_of_fallsAtLeast`); whole-number worlds are the instance with their own premises and nothing more (`World.rests_unrefuted_enclosed_whole`, `World.rests_unrefuted_whole`). Along any amount falling at every step a standing passing between two relata meets every other premise and never rests, its levels held by no list (`Pass.endless_keeps_from_rest`, `Pass.levels_unlisted`); so dyadic amounts (no least fall) and layered amounts (not Archimedean) each carry a world that never rests, and rest over every world in given amounts lies between no endless fall and none at every step (`Amounts.worldsRest_of_noEndlessFall`, `Amounts.not_endlessFall_of_worldsRest`; [rest-theorem-in-any-amounts](../results/records/rest-theorem-in-any-amounts.md), [rest-exactly-where-falls-stop](../results/records/rest-exactly-where-falls-stop.md)).
- *Openness* (`World.arising_opens`): in a counted, embodied, balanced and dissipative world, arising without end excludes being enclosed from any step on. The eight premises decide neither enclosure nor openness, and openness with them does not give arising without end (`exposed`, `holding`, `hatch`); a finite, enclosed, lossless world that never rests exists (`Passing.passes`).
- *Fed from beyond* (`Web.embodiedArising_fed_from_beyond`): a dissipative family enclosed around in which members keep arising cannot, from some step on, be fed from nothing beyond, in double-negation form; under the first law, more without end needs inflow from beyond every bounded part (`Flow.new_beyond_every`). Being fed needs no source to recur (`Successive.relay`). Used in §2.3 and §4.

Arising and ceasing are one part in finite worlds with decided activity, and the part in which every event is living crosses them (`World.living_crosses`): among finite worlds each recurrence pattern occurs both with every event living and with some event not living (`shuttle`, `grazing`, `mutualism`, `drawdown`). The refutation laws hold however a family is listed: 28 laws in refutation form need no listing once, six in `Becoming.Listing` and 22 as `_listing` corollaries (`Web.embodiedArising_fed_from_beyond_listing` among them); fixing every list's number up to indiscernibility in every world is excluded middle (`World.numbers_indiscernible_iff_em`). Open: the connection of listing to likeness of configurations (`Alike`).

*Readings* [AI reading, D2, D6, D43]. Theorems belong to the whole, premises to parts, and living is one part, not coinciding with arising. Arising and ceasing (生起, 止息) name activity starting and stopping; they are kept apart from “generative” and “degenerative”, which do not overlap, while arising and ceasing recurring coincide in finite worlds with decided activity. A count is a relation. The stepwise verdict on self-drawing at an ending is a theorem of the stepwise reading, and no comparison uses it.

### 1.4 The order of change and the step index

**In words.** What the theorems use of the shared index of steps is the order of change: exchanges whose footprints are disjoint commute, and only the partial order of joined exchanges shows. For exchanges acting locally the index is read off the relata's own courses, and beyond them it only orders exchanges that share no relatum. Two interleavings of the same courses are carried one to the other by one renumbering of all the steps, under which everything read at a step on what its exchange touches is the same; what stands together at a step across relata no exchange there relates is the interleaving's choice.

- `Footprinted.sorts_iff`: commuting neighbouring exchanges with disjoint footprints keeps every premise and reaches exactly the renumberings that keep the partial order of joined exchanges; stuttering and removing idle steps keep the premises, censuses and totals. A least footprint exists exactly where idleness is decided, and a normal form needs a decision that distinct exchanges are not joined (`Becoming.OrderNormal`).
- `run_of_courses`, `unique_iff`: an exchange acts locally where it leaves the relata it does not touch as they were and changes those it touches as their states determine (`LocalLaw`); what a relatum undergoes is its own course (`ownCourse`), and an index is a list interleaving the own courses (`Interleaves`). Two interleavings giving every relatum the same own course reach the same configuration; the own courses fix the interleaving exactly where every two exchanges are the same or share a relatum. A scheduler completes exactly where some interleaving exists (`schedule_iff`).
- `world_balances`, `world_swap`: from a local law the interleaving builds a `World` of the spine, each step balancing; two interleavings of the same own courses give every relatum the same course of standings (`standings_of_courses`), and swapping two adjacent exchanges that share no relatum changes the world only at the step between them.
- `world_index`, `world_reads_alike`: for any two interleavings of the same own courses, a relatum stands at a step of one world as it stands after its own exchanges up to that step (`world_own`), so it stands alike wherever it has taken as many of them (`world_of_own`); the index map sends a step of one list to the least step of the other with as many (`indexMap`, exact by `ownCount_index`), the relatum stands at step `t` of one world as at the mapped step of the other, and where it takes an exchange at step `t` the map sends `t + 1` to the step after the same exchange in the other list (`index_matches`). So whether a relatum has a standing at some step, or at every step, is the same in every interleaving. The relation is proved directly, not by composing adjacent swaps.
- `renumber_reindexes`, `world_renumber_reads`: where every exchange of the two interleavings touches a relatum (`Touching`), the `k`-th taking of an exchange in one is its `k`-th in the other at every relatum it touches (`occurrence_matches`), so one renumbering of all the steps (`renumber`) sends each step to the step of the other list taking the same exchange so, and on the steps of each relatum it touches it is that relatum's index map one step back (`renumber_index`). It is undone by the renumbering the other way and fixes every step from the common length on (`renumber_renumber`, `renumber_beyond`), so it `Reindexes` the steps in `Becoming.Reordering`'s sense. Through it activity agrees at every relatum (`world_renumber_active`), the step takes the same exchange (`world_renumber_flow`), and every relatum the exchange touches stands alike before and after it (`world_renumber_level`); so a relation reading those relata at one step, however many, is the same for every interleaving. A relation reading relata the step's exchange does not touch is not: in `Pair.apart_differs` one relatum stands below another at a step of one interleaving's world and at no step of the other's. An exchange touching no relatum is matched by none (`Pair.idle_unmatched`), which is why the premise is needed.
- `renumber_keeps`, `around_keeps_identity`: the renumbering keeps the order of exchanges sharing a relatum (`Keeps` for the footprint of the touches) and the footprint moves with it. That footprint contains the exchanges exactly where no relatum is touched at a step after one not touching it (`touch_contains_iff`; `Pair.touch_not_contains`), since the world's activity is taking part in the exchange across a step; the footprint around each step (what the exchange at it or at the next touches) contains them (`around_contains`) but joins every two consecutive exchanges, so the only renumbering keeping its order is none (`around_keeps_identity`). Reordering's commutations therefore relate the world of an interleaving to no other interleaving's world; the relation between them is the renumbering, and `world_renumber_correspondence` gathers on the worlds the premises of Reordering's `converse` it meets (the reindexing, the kept order, the same exchange at the renumbered step, the moved footprint); `Contains` it meets only as `touch_contains_iff` states.
- `OneStep.every_iff`: a pattern of one step is realized by a world exactly where it obeys the law of every step and of each named local condition; for families chosen step by step, running down adds continuity counted at positive levels (`OneStep.step_runsDown_iff`).
- `Weights.sum_courses`: given weights with their laws and finite lists, the sum over histories is exactly the stepwise transfer, and exact preservation through a description is one-step lumpability (`Weights.lumped_iff`); truncation, cancellation, unkept memory and loops each have boundary witnesses. Used in §2.6 (the orders of a carried difference are such a sum).

Not shown: a Foata or unbounded normal form; continuous time; separate footprints for reading and changing; courses without end; exchanges touching no relatum, which no renumbering matches; a reading of activity as a relatum's state between steps, under which Reordering's commutations could relate two interleavings' worlds; whether every becoming has a local law (§8.5); counts of the active and capacity over whole runs.

*Reading* [AI reading, D30, D43]. The shared index of steps is a reading of the relations, not their ground: where every two exchanges share a relatum it is derived, and elsewhere it is a commitment of the representation, a choice among interleavings the configurations do not read. What is read through each relatum's own steps does not depend on that choice; the choice moves where on the index a standing falls, not which standings a relatum passes through or in what order. Nor does what is read at one step on the relata its exchange relates; what the choice adds is only which relata no exchange relates stand together at a step.
